### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 澳洲 8 月就業人數勁增 3.95 萬人大幅優於預期，但勞動參與率上升至 67.1%，
推升失業率至 4.6%，呈現勞動力擴增但市場邊際降溫跡象。澳洲央行總裁布拉
克日前指出，失業率回升至5%有助於抑制頑固通膨。考量物價長期高於目標中
點，市場預期澳洲央行本週二將重啟緊縮，升息 1 碼至 4.60%，創 2011年底以
來新高。
 瑞士央行（SNB）上週四宣布連續第五次將關鍵政策利率維持在0%不變，符合
市場預期。儘管受油價推升使 8 月通膨微升至 0.8%，但仍處於目標區間，央行
預期中期通膨壓力溫和，現行極度寬鬆立場依然適當。決議公布後瑞郎兌美元跌
至 1.2077。相較全球主要央行相繼緊縮，瑞士央行維持超低利率，策略轉為視情
況調節匯市以穩定瑞郎。
 標普全球公布歐元區 9 月綜合 PMI 初值升至 53.1，創三年新高，製造與服務業
同步擴張，新訂單增速達四年多最快，印證經濟無懼美伊衝突引發的能源成本飆

升，展現超預期韌性。受能源價格傳導加速影響，通膨壓力再度升溫，儘管尚未
浮現薪資二輪效應，但強勁景氣數據促使市場大幅提高歐洲央行提前於10月例
會再度升息的押注。
 美國 9 月綜合 PMI 初值勁升至 58.4，創 62 個月新高；製造業與服務業 PMI 亦
逆勢走強至 57與 58.7，凸顯企業擴張強勁，引發市場對通膨持續性的擔憂。市
場焦點轉向 9 月 30日公布的美國 8 月 PCE、個人消費支出與 Q2 GDP終值，同
日日本亦將發布短觀調查與央行意見摘要。若終端消費延續強勁，將牽動美日央
行政策利率預期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.655，低點在 31.866，以 31.790作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 2.8分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，台股創新高，外資連續五日買超台股並匯入，推升
台幣最高來到 31.655，不過逢低吸引進口商、政府基金等買盤進場，台幣主要在
31.700 附近震盪；臨近周末，美國 9 月綜合 PMI 由 8 月的 56 走升至 58.4，創下逾
5 年來的新高，加上美伊戰事持續，皆推升市場對後續升息的預期，美元指數升至
101 上方，台幣跳空開低，回貶至 31.800 上方，最低來到 31.866，不過隨後出口商
賣壓出籠，台幣跌勢放緩，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.790。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國經濟表現仍具韌性，9月綜合
PMI 由 8 月的 56 上升至 58.4，企業活動加速至逾 5 年來最強，數據公布後美國 10
年債券殖利率升破 5%，Fed Watch 顯示市場對於聯準會 10 月升息預期升高，有助
於美元指數維持偏強格局，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，台股在創下
新高後回落，而外資也結束連續五日買超的走勢，轉為賣超，短線上資金匯入不利
台幣表現，然而，台灣8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6億美元、年增 71.4%，創歷史新高，
AI 需求帶動出口持續強勁，出口商後續拋匯需求預計將提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，
預估本週台幣區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走貶。人民幣中間價在上周連續十天走升，並升破6.7500整數
關口，最高來到 6.7468，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美伊戰事持續，市場對於聯準
會升息預期提升，美元指數震盪走強，加上週間公布美國9月綜合 PMI由 8 月的 56
走升至 58.4，創下逾 5 年來的新高，美元指數突破 101 整數關卡，令境外人民幣承
壓，USD/CNH回貶至 6.7200上方，終場境外人民幣收在6.7227，週線貶值283點，
境內人民幣則收在 6.7125，週線貶值 141 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣高檔震盪。就國際市場來看，美中兩國完成新一輪談判，美方
表示雙方同意將原訂 11 月 10 日到期的貿易休戰延長至明年 1 月 10 日，並持續就
關稅、貿易及供應鏈等議題進行協商，降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險，對人民幣形
成一定支撐。不過，美國近期經濟數據仍具韌性，加以聯準會偏鷹立場推升美元，
預計將限制人民幣升值空間；就中國國內市場來看，近期人行持續偏強設定中間價，
帶動人民幣維持偏強走勢，惟美中利差再度擴大，預計將限制人民幣升值的空間；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，匯價反彈至 20日均線之上，顯示原先下
行動能暫歇，上方壓力先看向季線 6.7350附近，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣高檔震
盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7400

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY先上後下。週初，美國多位聯準會官員鷹派談話，帶動美元指數
持續上行，推升 USD/JPY延續前一週的走勢來到 157上方；週間，受美國 PMI 強
於預期與美債殖利率全面上行帶動，市場重新提高對聯準會後續升息路徑的押注，
帶動 USD/JPY大漲突破 159的關卡；臨近週末，日本首相高市早苗表態日圓被低估
是個問題，使市場提高對政策訊號與潛在干預的敏感度，帶動USD/JPY回吐大部分
漲幅，終場收在 157.26，週線上漲 56點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本財務大臣片山皋月上週
五罕見透露，美國總統川普在美日元首峰會上對日圓過度疲弱表達關切，首相高市
早苗亦回應日圓遭低估確屬問題。儘管近期美元指數維持在 101 上方，但在日本政
府可能干預的陰影下，預計將限縮日圓貶值的空間。二、美國9 月綜合 PMI產出指
數創逾五年新高，疊加美伊戰事推升油價突破100美元與柴油大漲，通膨壓力劇增。
美聯儲理事巴爾表態可能需再升息兩次，芝加哥聯儲古爾斯比也示警能源衝擊具持
續性，推升市場押注 10 月升息機率升至六成，預計將支撐 USD/JPY 走勢。三、就
技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY連日上漲一度站回年線但隨即失守，目前依舊承
壓於季線及年線下方，不過各天期均線趨平，且下方尚有月線支撐，短線尚無明確
的方向。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.30-159.30 
前週交易區間 
156.56 159.03

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，歐元延續前一週偏弱的走勢，加上聯準會官員鷹
派發言，導致歐元跌破 1.1450的重要關卡；週間，儘管油價自高檔回落，但市場解
讀為能源成本仍處高位、難以迅速回落，推升對美國實質利率與利差優勢的預期。
加上歐洲在能源依賴與成長動能較弱下，相對於美國更難承受長期高利率環境，歐
元最低來到 1.1358；臨近週末，歐元跌深反彈，終場收在1.1391，週線下跌 91點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學家連
恩示警，第二波能源價格飆升將推升通膨，預計至2027年中期始回落至目標水準。
德國央行月報指出，受中東衝突、低天然氣庫存及明年醫療改革影響，通膨將居高
不下；夏季雖因出口疲弱與乾旱致增長趨緩，但在製造業回暖與財政支出挹注下，
估年底經濟有望重啟復甦，預計將提供歐元一定的支撐。二、美聯儲官員接連釋放
強烈鷹派信號，聖路易聯儲總裁穆薩萊姆直言當前 3.75%-4.00%利率仍偏寬鬆，呼
籲盡早收緊；芝加哥聯儲總裁古爾斯比亦警告，AI投資繁榮引發需求過熱，疊加持
續性供應衝擊，可能迫使美聯儲加速且更積極地升息。目前Fedwatch顯示預期明年
4 月前可能再升息三次，將不利歐元表現。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
在各天期均線下方，且接連失守1.15及 1.14整數關卡，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1300-1.1450 
前週交易區間 
1.1358 1.1495

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.21-115.09.24 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.804% ~ 0.830%  
30天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.69%  
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95%  
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.84%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本周央行持續每日減發定存單，令市場資金充裕，帶動短
率靠向區間低位。 
週二央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.638%，略低於
市場預期，且較上次標售結果下跌 2.5個基本點。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.804%~0.830%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.69%，利率較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 及 5 年期指標券 A15108 均未成
交，週四等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.95%、1.84%。 
台股排擠效應、國際債市走勢偏空，加上油價續處高檔，通膨潛在威脅仍存，
12 月升息可能性未能完全排除，市場氣氛續偏觀望，預期台債殖利率將維持
高檔區間盤整 ， 預估5年券區間為 1.84%~1.90%，10年券區間為 1.95%~2.0%。

ASE Technology Holding (3711.TW)
29 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Stable traditional businesses and EMS outlook — Traditional packaging and test
demand is improving, supported by utilization gains and a recovery in automotive
and industrial, as well as AI-adjacent chips. Management expects general-market
business to grow again in 2027, despite softer smartphone and PC demand. For
2027, ASEH expects further EMS growth, led by high-performance computing, and

is pursuing system-level solutions that combine EMS capabilities with ATM
technologies.
LEAP upside intact; GM upward trend structural
Following the solid 3Q26 result, it expects 4Q26 consolidated revenue to grow 6–
8% QoQ, with gross margin of 23–24% and operating margin of 14.5–15.5%. ATM
revenue is forecast to increase 11–13% QoQ, with GM improving 100–200 bps. EMS
revenue is expected to be flat QoQ, with an operating margin of about 2%.
Looking into 2027, management expects LEAP revenue to double, with further GM

improvement as LEAP ramps and utilization rises across traditional packaging and
test. Demand remains ahead of available capacity: assembly is expected to grow
faster than test, with investment prioritizing assembly and wafer probing. Full-
process revenue is projected to exceed US$1bn in 2027, while CPO is expected to
contribute more than US$500m. Capacity and facility space remain key
constraints; ASE plans to increase capex to US$11.5bn in 2027 given its raised

2026 outlook. It also expects traditional-market growth to continue, supported by
automotive, industrial and AI-adjacent demand, despite softer smartphone and PC
markets.
On the back of its structural GM upside and more LEAP revenue contribution, we
lift our 2026/2027 /2028 earnings projection by 12%/18%/16%. We maintain Buy
with TP NT$908, based on an unchanged 25x 2027 EPS.

### Page 8

ASE Technology Holding (3711.TW)
29 October 2026 Citi Research
8
ASE Technology Holding
Company description
Advanced Semiconductor Engineering (ASE), incorporated in 1984 and listed
at the TWSE in 1989, is one of the world's leading companies in the
semiconductor packaging and testing sector. In June 2016, ASE entered into a
Joint Share Exchange Agreement with SPIL under which ASE Industrial was
eventually to be formed as a holding company with 100% equity interests in

both ASE and SPIL. ASE Industrial was listed on Taiwan's main board on April
30, 2018, the same day on which ASE and SPIL were delisted.
Investment strategy
We have a Buy rating on ASE Holding. The combined ASE-SPIL entity is the
highest-quality OSAT worldwide, in our view, with its market share
leadership, superior operations, economies of scale, and customer/product
diversification. Conditions imposed by the Chinese government on the ASE-
SPIL merger, which required them to operate independently, were lifted in

2020. Consequently, we believe ASE Holding will benefit from further revenue
and operating synergies as well as the trend for advanced packaging.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$908 is based on 25x 2027 EPS average. Our PER is
higher than its five-year range as we expect ASEH to see accelerating
earnings growth in the coming years with GM at upward expansion trend,
mainly driven by its promising outlook in LEAP business and strong expansion
for long-term AI demand. At our target price, the shares would trade at a

2026/27E P/B of 10x/8x.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent ASE shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) poor efficiency and less synergy created from merger with
SPIL; 2) failure to gain market share in advanced packaging; 3) industry
oversupply due to aggressive capacity expansion; 4) severe pricing erosion
due to competition; and 5) a longer-than-expected semiconductor inventory
correction.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned

Yageo (2327.TW)

Yageo
Company description
Established in 1977 and listed in 1993, Yageo is a leading supplier of passive
electronic components based in Taiwan. The company offers a wide array of
products including MLCC, chip resistors, magnetics, tantalum, and other
components, aiming to be the total solution provider for its customers. By
capacity, the company currently ranks No. 1 in chip resistor and tantalum and

No. 3 in MLCC and inductor globally. Key customers of Yageo include leading
EMS, ODMs, and distributors. The company also has 29 sales offices, 51
manufacturing sites, and 20 R&D centers in 29 nations worldwide.
Investment strategy
We have a Buy rating on Yageo shares. Yageo is widely known as a passive
electronic component supplier. Although passive components are generally
viewed as a commodity, Yageo is no longer a commodity play, in our view,

given the company’s foray into premium markets through M&As, which
should help mitigate the impact of sector cyclicality and order volatility. We
expect a more diversified product portfolio to drive stable earnings and better
margins, while improving utilization in its commodity component business
following the end of inventory digestions should add additional fuel.
Valuation
We set our target price at NT$1,000, based on 23x average 2027E-28E
earnings. We value Yageo based on PE as we believe the company’s earnings

performance and industry dynamics would be the key focuses of the market.
Our target multiple of 23x is at the higher end of the trading range since 2022
but in line with global peers average. During this period the company has gone
through the cycles, successfully integrated the acquired business to enhance
its product offerings, and started to deliver stable margins and earnings.
Risks
Key downside risks to our target price and rating include: 1) slower-than-

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be

Document 250: AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか ～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか ～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026年10月 29日

1/8 
AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか 
～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ 日銀は 2026年 10月の金融システムレポートで、人工知能（AI）関連投資への期待が縮小し
た場合の金融安定上のリスクを分析した。本稿では同レポートに基づき日本へのリスクの波
及経路を整理したうえで、金融機関のストレス耐性を確認する。 
○ 米国の巨大IT企業によるAI関連投資が急拡大している。これらの企業は従来の自己資金に
加え、社債発行などによる外部資金調達を増やしている。データセンター事業では、長期リ
ース契約を裏付けとした銀行融資や、プライベートファンドによる投融資が広がっている。
AI関連の旺盛な資金需要は経済成長につながる一方、特定分野へのリスクの集中と表裏一体
の関係にある。 
○ 日本への影響の波及経路として、銀行融資、金融市場、実体経済の三つが考えられる。日本
の大手行は海外のデータセンター事業者やファンドへの融資を増やしている。また、AI関連
株の下落をきっかけにヘッジファンドが投資ポジションを縮小すれば、日本の株式市場だけ
でなく、国債市場にも影響が及ぶ可能性がある。AI関連投資の減速によるアジア経済の悪化
も、日本の金融機関の信用コスト増加につながり得る。 
○ 日銀のストレステストでは、AI関連投資への期待の縮小とノンバンクによるショックの増幅
を想定しても、日本の金融機関は相応の耐性を備えているとの結果となった。一方、AI関連
企業の間では出資と将来の売上高の約束が結びついた循環的な取引がみられ、その影響はハ
イパースケーラーを通じて日本の大手行のデータセンター向け融資にも間接的に及び得る。
取引の連鎖全体を見る視点が必要だ。

1. はじめに 
日銀は10月23日、金融システムレポートを公表した。IMFが先に公表した国際金融安定性報告
書（GFSR）と同様、金融安定上のリスク要因として人工知能（AI）に焦点を当てた。AI関連企業
の成長期待を背景に、世界の株式市場では株価上昇が続き、データセンターなどへの設備投資も
拡大している。一方、投資規模が膨らむにつれて、巨額の投資に見合った収益が実現するかどう
かが金融市場の大きな関心事となっている。

### Page 2

2/8 
今回、注目したいのはAI関連投資と海外のノンバンク金融仲介機関（NBFI）の動向だ。AI関連
投資への期待が縮小した場合、海外ノンバンクの資産売却などを通じてショックが増幅され、日
本の金融市場にも波及する可能性を日銀は指摘している。本稿では、日銀の分析をもとに、AI関
連投資を支える資金調達の変化と、海外ノンバンクを通じた日本へのリスクの波及経路を整理し
たうえで、金融機関のストレス耐性を確認する。

2. 拡大するAI投資とノンバンクの役割 
AI関連投資は近年、大幅に増えている。日銀の図表によると、米国の巨大IT企業（ハイパース
ケーラー）5社の設備投資額は、2022年頃の四半期約400億ドルから2026年 4～6月期には約
1,800億ドルと4倍以上に増加した（注1） 。これらの企業は従来、潤沢な営業キャッシュフロー
をもとにデータセンター投資を行ってきた。ところが、足元では社債発行などによる外部資金調
達を増やすようになった。投資額の大きいデータセンターを例に見ていこう。 
ハイパースケーラーでは、自社でデータセンターを建設するだけでなく、外部事業者と長期リ
ース契約を結んで利用権を確保する動きも目立っている。5社の未開始のリース契約額は2026年
4～6月期に約1.1兆ドルに達した（資料1、注1）。

資料 1 米ハイパースケーラー5社の設備投資と資金調達

（注）集計対象はAlphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、Oracleの 5社。①の「ネット借入・社債発行」と「ネット株式発行」は、プラス
が資金調達。直近は 2026年 4～6月期。数値は日本銀行の図表から筆者が読み取った概数。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-8（原資料はLSEG Workspace、SEC）をもとに第一ライフ資
産運用経済研究所作成

データセンターを建設・保有する事業者は、ハイパースケーラーとの長期リース契約を裏付け
に、銀行などから建設資金を調達している。銀行が融資を組成し、その貸出債権をプライベート
ファンドが購入する事例や、ファンドがデータセンター事業に直接出資する事例もある。こうし
て、AI関連投資を支える資金の出し手として、銀行に加えてプライベートファンドなどのNBFIの

### Page 3

3/8 
存在感が高まっている。 
日銀が集計した2023年1月から 2026年8月までに公表された米国のデータセンター案件で
は、資金調達額は合計約1.6兆ドルに上る。このうち調達済みは約0.2兆ドルにとどまり、約1.4
兆ドルが計画段階にある。また、案件数の約2割でプライベートファンドがスポンサーとなって
いる（注2）。 
さらに、AI関連企業の間では、出資と将来の売上高が結びついた循環的な取引もみられる。IMF
が2026年10月に公表したGFSRによると、大手IT企業などが財務基盤の相対的に弱いAI企業に出
資し、出資を受けた企業が出資元などにサービスや半導体を発注する事例がある。IMFが確認した
2023年以降の14案件では、約3,000億ドルの出資と約6,500億ドルの債務調達が、約1.4兆ドル
の将来の売上高の約束と結びついている。出資先の経営が悪化すれば、将来の売上高と投融資の
価値が同時に損なわれる可能性がある。 
こうした資金調達の拡大によって、AI関連投資と銀行、プライベートファンドなどの結びつき
が強まっている。AI関連投資が期待どおりの収益を生まなければ、データセンター事業者への融
資や出資を通じて、銀行やファンドに損失が生じる可能性がある。さらに、銀行はファンド自体
にも融資しており、ファンドの財務状況の悪化が銀行に波及する経路もある。日銀は、こうした
金融主体間の結びつきを通じて、ショックが金融システムに広がる可能性を警戒している。

3. 日本に及ぶ三つの波及経路 
AI関連投資の収益性に対する懸念の高まりや投資そのものの減速に直面した場合、日本の金融
システムにはどのような影響が及ぶのか。日銀の分析をもとに、①銀行融資、②金融市場、③実
体経済――の三つの波及経路からみていく。

（1） 銀行の融資を通じた経路 
日本の大手行は海外でデータセンター事業者やプライベートファンドへの融資を増やしてい
る。日銀によると、2026年 3月末時点で3メガ行の海外貸出全体に占めるデータセンター向けの
割合は 3％、海外ファンド向けは9％だった。また、3メガ行の海外ファンド向け貸出残高は12兆
円と、前年同月末から29.2％増加した。海外貸出全体に占める割合は現時点で限定的だが、収益
性の高さなどから注力分野となっている。データセンター事業者には賃料収入を特定の巨大IT企
業に依存するケースがあり、AI投資が期待どおりの収益を生まなければ、融資の返済能力にも影
響しかねない。また、技術革新によって高性能半導体などの設備が短期間で陳腐化するリスクも
ある。 
海外ファンド向け融資も注視すべきだろう。銀行はファンドに直接融資しているほか、ファン
ドが投資する企業や事業にも資金を供給している。ファンド向け貸出は、信用力の高い投資家の
出資約束を担保とする形式が多く、投資適格の比率は高い。ただ、日銀は、海外ファンドの運用
実態や資産価値を適時に把握するのは必ずしも容易ではないと指摘する。市場で大きなショック
が生じれば、ファンドが契約済みの融資枠から資金を引き出し、銀行の資金供給負担が増える可

### Page 4

4/8 
能性もある。

（2） 金融市場を通じた経路 
日本株の上昇は、AI関連需要の恩恵が相対的に大きい電機、精密、非鉄、機械などの業種がけ
ん引してきた。日銀は、AI関連投資に見合った収益拡大が実現しない場合、日本株にも調整圧力
が生じ得ると指摘しており、相応の株式を保有する金融機関にとってもリスクとなる。 
さらに、日銀によると、世界の株式市場に投資するヘッジファンドでは、AI関連銘柄の保有割
合が高まっている。マクロ戦略を採るヘッジファンドでは、個別株式の保有額に占める米大手ハ
イテク 7社の割合が、2019年の約14％から2026年には約23％に上昇した（注3） 。こうしたファ
ンドのなかには、借り入れやデリバティブを活用して投資規模を拡大し、株式だけでなく国債な
どにも投資するものがある。AI関連株が急落すれば、損失の拡大や追加の証拠金請求などを受け
て、株式以外の投資ポジションも縮小する可能性がある。 
この影響が及び得る市場の一つが、日本の国債市場だ。ヘッジファンドを含む海外投資家は、
日本国債の現物と先物、金利スワップなどの価格差を利用した裁定取引を拡大させているとみら
れる。日銀によると、国債現物の対顧客取引に占める海外投資家の割合は、2015年の約3割から
2025年には6割強に上昇した（注4）。 
AI関連株の下落をきっかけにファンドがレバレッジを縮小すれば、国債関連のポジション解消
が進み、債券価格や金利の変動につながる可能性がある。日銀は、海外投資家による取引の増加
が市場の流動性や価格形成に寄与する場合がある一方、海外市場のショックが証拠金の請求など
をきっかけにポジション解消を引き起こし、日本の金融市場に影響を与えやすくなっている可能
性を指摘している。

（3） 実体経済を通じた経路 
日銀はストレステストで、ハイパースケーラーなどのAI関連投資が失速した場合、AI関連産業
の比重が高いアジア経済が大きく減速すると想定している。日本でも、情報通信機械、はん用・
生産用機械、電気機械などのAI関連産業を中心に経済が減速する。企業収益の悪化は、アジア向
けを中心とする貸出の信用コスト増加を通じて、金融機関に跳ね返る。 
AI関連投資を巡る三つの波及経路は相互に関連している。設備投資の減速による企業収益の悪
化は、株価の下落や信用リスクの上昇にもつながり得る。日本と海外NBFIの結びつきは、すでに
相当な規模に達している。日本の銀行による海外NBFIへの投融資残高は、2008年 3月末の8兆円
から 2026年6月末には126兆円に増加した。海外の投資ファンド等による日本の株式・債券への
投資残高も、同期間に111兆円から408兆円へ拡大している（資料2）。このうち株式が355兆円
を占め、債券は53兆円にとどまる（注5） 。いずれもAI関連に限った金額ではないが、海外の金融
市場で発生したショックが日本に波及する経路の広がりを示している。

### Page 5

5/8

資料 2 日本の金融部門と海外ノンバンクの結びつき

（注）左図は国内銀行等による海外ノンバンクへの投融資残高。右図は、日銀がIMFの部門別対外証券投資データなどを用いて
試算した、海外主体による日本向け証券投資残高のうち「投資ファンド等」の値で、ディーラー・ブローカーやファイナンス会
社などを含む。海外ノンバンク全体の日本向け証券投資残高は 507兆円（2026年 6月末）。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-2（原資料はBIS、IMF、U.S. Department of the Treasury、
日本銀行）をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

4. 複合ショックにも耐性を示す日本の金融機関 
前章で整理した銀行融資、金融市場、実体経済の三つの波及経路に沿って、日本の金融機関へ
の影響をみていこう。日銀は銀行105行、信用金庫247庫を対象に、2028年度末までの財務状況
を試算するマクロ・ストレステストを実施した。本稿で確認する「海外金利上昇シナリオplus」
では、AI関連投資の収益性に対する期待の縮小に加え、内外のNBFIによる資産売却などを通じて
金融市場の混乱が増幅される状況を想定した。 
まず、銀行融資を通じた波及経路を確認する。海外のデータセンター事業者やプライベートク
レジット（PC）ファンド向けの貸出が傷むというシナリオを置いた。両者に対する貸出の信用コ
スト率が最大約10ポイント悪化する想定だ。もっとも、国際統一基準行の信用コスト増加に占め
る海外ファンド・データセンター向けの寄与は限定的だった。 
金融市場を通じた経路では、株価や債券価格の大幅な下落を想定した。米国ではAI関連銘柄に
ITバブル崩壊後の下落率を当てはめるなどして、米国株価がベースラインから最大約50％下落す
るとした。日本株も最大約40％の下落を見込む。また、NBFIによるショックの増幅を織り込み、
米国のBBB格社債の信用スプレッドは最大約1,100ベーシスポイント拡大する。日米の長期金利も
ベースラインより1.5ポイント上昇する。こうした株価と債券価格の同時下落によって、金融機
関の有価証券評価損益などが悪化する。また、海外金利の上昇に伴う外貨調達コストの増加が、
海外の資金利益を押し下げる。 
実体経済を通じた経路では、ハイパースケーラーなどのAI関連投資の失速によって、関連産業

### Page 6

6/8 
の比重が高いアジア経済が大きく減速すると想定した。日銀の試算では、アジアの実質GDPはベー
スラインから最大約7％下振れする。日本でも関連製造業を中心に企業収益が悪化し、銀行の信用
コスト増加につながる。今回のシナリオには原油高などの影響も含まれており、信用コストの増
加には国内向け貸出の寄与が大きい。 
こうした複合的なショックを想定しても、日銀の試算では金融機関の自己資本比率は全体とし
て規制水準を上回った。2028年度末の国際統一基準行の自己資本比率は、ベースラインの12.8％
から 8.9％に低下する（資料3）。

資料 3 マクロ・ストレステストにおける 2028年度末の自己資本比率

（注）対象は銀行 105行と信用金庫 247庫。「金融調整シナリオ」は、内外の金融市場や実体経済にリーマンショック並みの大幅
な調整が生じる想定。「海外金利上昇シナリオplus」は、内外金利の上昇、原油価格の上昇、AI関連投資の収益性に対する
期待の縮小などが同時に生じ、ノンバンク部門によるショックの増幅も織り込んだ想定。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅵ-2-5、Ⅵ-2-7をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

この水準はリーマンショック並みの市場調整を想定した「金融調整シナリオ」の10.1％を下回
る。今回のシナリオの影響の大きさが分かる。一方、国内基準行の銀行は10.4％から8.8％、信
用金庫は14.0％から13.2％への低下にとどまった。国内基準行では、金融調整シナリオ（銀行
8.6％、信用金庫12.6％）の方が低下幅は大きい。 
日銀は、AI関連投資への期待の縮小などがNBFIを通じて増幅される複合的なショックに対して
も、「金融システムの安定性は全体として維持される」と評価している。ただ、試算結果は業態ご
との平均値で、個々の金融機関の自己資本比率にはばらつきがある点には留意が必要だろう。

5. おわりに 
日銀のストレステストでは、AI関連投資への期待の縮小やNBFIを通じたショックの増幅を織り
込んでも、日本の金融機関は全体として相応の耐性を備えているとの結果となった。一方、AI関

### Page 7

7/8 
連企業間の循環的な取引が、損失を増幅させる可能性にも注目したい。 
現在のAI関連企業の資金調達の特徴として、資金と売上が企業間で相互に結びつく関係があげ
られる。この循環的な取引を拙稿「AIと金融市場、強まる相互依存～IMFの国際金融安定性報告書
から読み解く新たなリスク～」で指摘した。本稿の2章でも述べた通り、大手企業が財務基盤の
相対的に弱いAI企業に出資し、出資先が見返りとして大手企業のサービスや半導体を将来購入す
ることを約束する仕組みだ。IMFのGFSRによると、約6,500億ドルの債務のうち約3,200億ドルは
出資先が負っており、そこには出資元の保証付きの債務やオフバランスの債務も含まれる。出資
先の経営が悪化すれば、出資元の将来の売上高が減少すると同時に、出資や融資の価値も損なわ
れる可能性がある。IMFはこれを「誤方向リスク（wrong-way risk） 」と呼び、循環的な取引が損
失を増幅するリスクを評価するよう監督当局に求めている。 
筆者は、日本の金融機関もこの構造と無縁ではないと考える。データセンター事業者は、ハイ
パースケーラーとの長期リース契約を裏付けに、銀行などから資金を調達している。IMFによる
と、循環的な取引の出資を主導しているのはハイパースケーラーだ。出資先のAI企業の経営が悪
化すれば、取引相手となるハイパースケーラーの将来の売上高が下振れし、データセンターの需
要見通しにも影響が及ぶ可能性がある。そうなれば、ハイパースケーラーとのリース契約を裏付
けとする日本の大手行のデータセンター向け融資も、間接的に影響を受ける可能性がある。表面
上は信用力の高いハイパースケーラーとの契約に見えても、その先に循環的な取引が組み込まれ
ていれば、リスクは見かけより高いのではないか。 
日本の金融機関のデータセンター向けやファンド向けの貸出は、現時点では海外貸出の一部に
とどまる。それでも、損失が特定の企業群にとどまらず、連鎖的に広がっていくリスクには目を
配っておきたい。AI関連投資が持続的な収益を生み出せるかに加え、企業間の出資と取引の結び
つきが、銀行やNBFIの資金供給を通じて金融システムにどう波及するのか。その把握には、個々
の融資先の信用力だけでなく、取引の連鎖全体を見る視点が必要になるだろう。

以 上

Document 252: Anthropic招股書裡最值得看的14個細節

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic招股書裡最值得看的14個細節.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。 
Anthropic 招股書裡最值得看的 14 個細節 
2026/10/27

摘要： 
⚫ 華爾街見聞整理了人工智慧獨角獸 Anthropic 提交 S-1 招股書的核心數據與關鍵細節。公司
擬以代碼「ANT」登陸那斯達克，目標估值介於 1.5 兆至 2 兆美元之間。 
⚫ 營收五個季度漲了約 30 倍，第二季已經轉盈，上半年帳面卻虧了 807 億美元，算力帳單還簽
到了 2045 年……Anthropic 招股書裡最值得看的 14 個細節，不少都帶著這樣的反差。更少見
的是控制權：普通股東幾乎沒有話語權，50.1%的投票權握在七位創辦人手中。 
1. 瘋狂的營收增速與放緩跡象  
⚫ 營收從 2023 年的 4,100 萬美元飆升至 2025 年的 45.85 億美元，2026 上半年達 
162.86 億美元；第二季單季營收 115.55 億美元，五個季度內暴增約 30 倍。今年前 9 
個月累計收入突破 330 億美元，過去一年人均創收達 660 萬美元。不過第二季至第三季
營收季增幅已放緩至接近 50%，公司警示早期爆發增長後未來可能停滯。

### Page 2

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。 
2. 半年帳面虧損 807 億美元，主因為公允價值變動  
⚫ 2026 上半年帳面淨虧損達 807.48 億美元，但其中約 723.88 億美元是因公司估值大幅
上升，導致可轉換票據、衍生負債與 SAFE 評估價值提高所認列的帳面虧損，並無現金流
出。扣除股權激勵等非現金費用後，上半年調整後營業利潤為 16.18 億美元，調整後淨利
潤達 26.71 億美元。 
3. 算力成本佔比驟降，經營利潤成功轉正  
⚫ 每 1 美元營收對應的算力與基礎設施支出，從 2025 年第一季的超過 2.5 美元降至 
2026 年第二季的不足 50 美分（算力支出佔比由 2023 年的 10 倍以上降至 2026 上
半年的 55%）。2026 年第二季經營利潤達 9.74 億美元（利潤率 8%），上半年營運現
金流淨流入 39 億美元，主要受惠於「閉環研發」模型訓練效率提升、推理優化、跨晶片
自建調度及降價策略。

4. 客戶結構多元化，頭部集中度下降  
⚫ 今年前 9 個月前五大客戶貢獻 25% 營收，但佔比從第一季的約 36% 降至第三季的 
21%，已無單一客戶佔比超過 10%。年消費破 10 萬美元的客戶超過 11,900 家，超過
半數名列財富 500 強（但其支出佔自身總預算不到 0.1%）。淨收入留存率（不含訂閱）
高達約 900%，且第三季有超過 80% 的營收來自近 6 個月內發布的新模型。

### Page 3

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。

5. 5%至 7%的收入來自違規客戶  
⚫ 前 9 個月約有 5% 至 7%（約 16 億至 23 億美元）的營收來自事後被判定違反使用規
範或商業條款的客戶，主因是監控、調查至封號存在時間差。隨著 8、9 月加大封禁力
度，此類收入佔比將下降，可能對未來的營收與增速造成一定拉低。 
6. 算力長約承諾超 8,000 億美元，最遠鎖定至 2045 年  
⚫ 各項固定或保底採購、已簽約未起算之租約及後續長期合約合計超過 8,300 億美元，包括
博通（五年 1,610 億美元租約及後續最高 1,500 億美元支出）、AMD（逾 200 億美
元）、三大雲端巨頭（十年至少 2,694 億美元）等。合約多採能源長約常見的「照付不
議」（用量不足仍須補足差額）條款，成本提前鎖定，而公司 80% 收入卻採依用量實際
結算，形成節奏差異。

### Page 4

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。
7. 每月支付給馬斯克的 SpaceX 算力費高達 31 億美元
⚫ Anthropic 租用 SpaceX.AI（原 xAI）位於孟菲斯的資料中心，合約總額最高達 1,205
億美元，合約至 2029 年。12 月算力全面上線後每月費用約 31 億美元，該月費已接近
SpaceX 今年首季整個 AI 業務營收（8.18 億美元）的四倍之多。
8. 巨頭持股版圖與極短的解禁安排
⚫ 若以 2 兆美元估值計算，亞馬遜全面轉股後約持股近 20%（約 4,000 億美元，超出
12% 上限部分暫以票據保留），Google 持股約 7% 並擁有後續增資與基石投資權限。
解禁期不同於傳統 180 天限制，上市後 76 天內設五個解禁日，首日即允許非創辦人董
事、高管與部分員工減持套現。
9. 特殊雙層治理架構，創辦團隊握有 50.1%絕對投票權
⚫ 普通股東對決策幾乎無控制權，亞馬遜與 Google 持有的 N 類股亦無投票權。公司頂層

由「長期利益信託」（LTBT）掌控制衡，而七位共同創辦人則透過僅 1 股、每人出資 1
美元認購的「F 類股」掌握 50.1% 的絕對投票權，屬於那斯達克規則下的「受控公
司」。
10. 股價每漲 50%，最高提撥 2%股權投入公益
⚫ 上市前已將全面攤薄股本的 5%（約 8.47 億股 N 類股）劃入 Anthropic 公益基金。董
事會批准未來股價若從發行價起每上漲 50%，可再額外注入最高 2% 股份；同時七位共
同創辦人承諾將個人 80% 的股權收益用於公益捐贈。
11. 長達 90 多頁的「生存級」風險因子揭露
⚫ 篇幅約為 SpaceX 的三倍，直接提及技術可能對人類構成「災難性或生存級風險」、可能
被惡意行為者用於開發致命武器，甚至可能衍生出模型抵制關機、操縱資訊或勒索等失控
行為。招股書直言，此類系統性風險恐怕連整個全球保險業都無力承保。
12. 創辦團隊向投資者發出的理念警告
⚫ 七位創辦人公開呼籲投資人切勿純以短期財務交易視角看待公司，直言部分商業決策會在
公共利益與利潤最大化之間權衡，作法可能與股東預期背道而馳。同時，團隊也著重強調

創始成員至今未流失，與競爭對手 OpenAI 形成對比。
13. 上市股票代碼為「ANT」，展現低自我企業文化
⚫ Anthropic 將以股票代碼「ANT」在那斯達克掛牌。招股書以「low-ego」（低自我）形
容內部文化，員工內部自稱為「Ants」（螞蟻），形成文化與代碼的巧妙呼應。

### Page 5

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。 
14. 市場下一步檢驗指標：10 月 ARR 表現  
⚫ 市場高度關注 S-1 補充文件中將揭露的 10 月 Run-Rate Revenue（以近 28 天用量年
化加計訂閱費）。目前市場預期 730 億美元為合理基準，若突破 780 億美元，將大幅提
高市場對其在 12 月達成 1,000 億美元 ARR 的信心，成為左右其 1.5 兆至 2 兆美元 
IPO 估值的關鍵密碼。

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations

### Page 3

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

Document 312: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.
Article completed on 14 September 2026
Lucie Barette
lucie.barette@bnpparibas.com

Document 361: Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

13030420     1

Transformation 
Quantum dictionary, part 1: Decoding the fundamentals 
21 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• What is quantum computing? How does it work? What is a qubit? And when will quantum reach its full potential? Quantum 
computing challenges our intuition and our understanding of what computers can do. In this two-part dictionary series, we 
decode key terms behind what could be the next major technological revolution. 
• Qubits are the basic building blocks of quantum computers, harnessing properties such as superposition and entanglement to 
process information. However, qubits are highly sensitive to their surroundings where disturbances can lead to errors in 
quantum computation, making error correction a key challenge for building quantum computers. 
• There is no single approach to building a quantum computer. Researchers and companies are pursuing a range of qubit 
technologies, from neutral atoms and photons to spin, superconducting, topological and trapped-ion qubits, while quantum 
computing itself is likely to increasingly work alongside classical computing and AI.

First, let’s lay the groundwork… 
Quantum computing  
Quantum computing uses qubits and quantum properties such as superposition (defined later on) to perform complex 
calculations and solve certain problems faster than classical computers.1 From a practical perspective, quantum computing could 
be used to tackle problems related to optimization, machine learning, simulation and factorization across a range of sectors, 
including autos and logistics, financial services, energy and climate, health and life sciences and national security. 
Current quantum computers can perform ‘just’ thousands of quantum operations. As the technology advances from thousands to 
millions, billions and eventually trillions of operations, quantum computers could begin to have commercial applications.  
• Millions: Financial services and security could experience some of the first use cases, such as threat detection, fraud 
prevention, derivatives pricing, bond trading and arbitrage. 
• Billions: Quantum could help power AI through advanced data generation capabilities.  
• Trillions: Quantum chemistry applications could emerge, including drug discovery, battery simulation and materials 
discovery. 
These are the publicly known applications, according to Oxford Quantum Circuits (OQC). And as quantum computing capabilities 
improve, some of these use cases could emerge sooner than anticipated.  
Now, we’ll tackle the core concepts… 
Qubits 
Qubits (quantum bits) are a quantum computer’s basic unit of information. They can exhibit quantum properties such as 
superposition and entanglement (which we elaborate on below) — properties fundamental to quantum computing. 
Quantum gates manipulate the state of qubits, much like logic gates manipulate bits in classical computers. Despite the name, a 
quantum gate is not a physical object, but rather an operation applied to a quantum system that changes its state. 
From physical to logical qubits 
Physical qubits are the foundation of a quantum computer’s hardware. However, they are highly susceptible to error. To address 
this, multiple physical qubits can be structured together to encode a logical qubit, allowing errors to be detected and corrected

1 Schneider, J., & Smalley, I. (2025, June 10). What is quantum computing? IBM. 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

and making the stored quantum information more reliable. In recent years, according to BofA Global Research, logical qubits 
have become a greater focus for quantum computing companies. 
Entanglement 
Entanglement is a condition where two or more qubits become linked so that their states are correlated, regardless of distance 
(Exhibit 1). It allows quantum computers to process information in ways that can be difficult or impossible for classical 
computers. 
So, how does it work? Suppose we have two photons that have opposite polarizations (the orientation of their light waves). 
Before measurement, neither has a definite polarization. When one is measured as horizontally polarized, the other will be 
vertically polarized when measured in the same basis, wherever it is. When measured separately, they appear to be random, but 
when compared, they are correlated (Exhibit 2). 
The challenges 
Once created, entanglement must be protected from decoherence (where interactions with the environment destroy quantum 
correlations). Maintaining entanglement over long distances is another challenge. Photons traveling through optical fiber can be 
absorbed or scattered, making long-distance quantum communication difficult. 2 Quantum repeaters aim to overcome this 
challenge by extending entanglement across a series of shorter links. 
Exhibit 1: Entanglement is a state where at least two qubits become 
correlated

determines the state of the other, no matter the distance

Superposition 
Unlike a classical bit, which can be one of two states (0 or 1), superposition allows a quantum system to exist in multiple states 
or configurations at the same time (Exhibit 3). For example, a qubit can exist in the 0 state, the 1 state or any state that is a 
combination of 0 and 1.3  
A quantum system can maintain a superposition until it is observed or measured. Once measured, its superposition collapses into 
a single state. Another way of thinking about superposition is to imagine a sphere with the north pole representing zero and the 
south pole representing one. Other points on the surface of the sphere represent different superposition states. This allows us 
to: 1) represent complex forms of information; and 2) simulate atoms in a computer. 
Schrödinger’s cat 
In 1935, physicist Erwin Schrödinger proposed a thought experiment to illustrate the strange implications of quantum 
mechanics. In this scenario, a cat, a radioactive substance that decays very slowly, and a Geiger counter are put together in a 
sealed box. According to quantum mechanics, the system can be described as a superposition in which the cat is both alive and 
dead until the box is opened. Once the box is opened and the system is observed, the superposition collapses into a single 
outcome.

Enabling parallel computation versus classical computing 
Superposition is a key attribute of quantum computing that allows for quantum parallelism, or the ability to perform multiple 
computations at the same time. Classical bits can only be in one of two states, 0 or 1. In contrast, a qubit can be in a 
superposition of 0 and 1, allowing a quantum computer to process multiple possible qubit states at once.4  
Exhibit 3: Superposition is the condition where a qubit can exist in a superposition of 0 and 1 
Illustrating the power of superposition

Note: A Bloch sphere is a geometric representation of qubit states. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Error rates and fidelity 
Fidelity is a measure of how closely a quantum state or operation matches what was intended (Exhibit 4). A higher fidelity 
generally means fewer errors. 
Physical qubits are prone to errors. One way to address this is to combine multiple physical qubits to create a more reliable 
logical qubit. Quantum error correction (QEC) allows errors in the physical qubits to be detected and corrected, helping protect 
the information stored in the logical qubit.

4 Ibid.

### Page 4

4 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 4: Fidelity measures how accurately a qubit retains its intended quantum state during computation 
Infographic showing the implications of high versus low fidelity

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
How does quantum error correction work? 
At its simplest, QEC relies on redundancy, or storing information across multiple qubits so that errors can be detected and 
corrected.  
Consider an example: suppose someone wants to send a message represented by 0. Instead of sending a single 0, the message 
could be repeated three times as 000, known as a codeword. If one bit were accidentally flipped from 0 to 1 during transmission, 
the recipient might receive 010. Because two of the three bits are still 0, the recipient could infer that the intended message 
was 000. 
However, if two bits flipped, producing 110 or 011, the recipient could infer the wrong message. If all three flipped to 111, the 
recipient would have no way to know from the received codeword that errors had occurred.5 This approach is known as a 
repetition code and illustrates how redundancy can help protect information from errors.  
Repetition codes are a simple form of error correction that can address bit-flip errors, where a 0 changes to a 1 or vice versa. 
More sophisticated QEC codes are needed to address other quantum errors, such as phase flips.6 Shor’s code, developed by 
American theoretical computer scientist Peter Shor, uses nine physical qubits to encode one logical qubit and can correct one 
bit-flip error or one phase flip error, but it can’t correct both types of errors at the same time. Meanwhile, Steane’s seven-qubit 
code, developed by Oxford physicist Andrew Steane, can also correct both types of errors. It has the advantage of being fault-
tolerant, meaning the error-correction process itself does not introduce additional errors.7 
At what error level might we start seeing quantum advantage? 
Some classical computing systems today operate with extremely high reliability, often described as having "17 nines" of fidelity 
(99.99999999999999999%). By comparison, quantum computers currently achieve fidelity rates of around 99.9%. Gerald 
Mullally, CEO of OQC, has argued that quantum systems would need to add roughly three more nines of fidelity to achieve 
commercial relevance, or quantum advantage. 
Noise and decoherence 
Noise refers to unwanted disturbances that affect quantum systems and can lead to errors in computations (Exhibit 5). It can 
come from sources such as interactions with the environment, electromagnetic interference and temperature fluctuations. 
Coherence is a quantum system’s ability to maintain its quantum properties, including superposition and entanglement. When 
coherence is lost through interactions with the environment, the process is known as “decoherence,” causing the system to 
behave more classically (Exhibit 6).8

5 Microsoft Ignite. (n.d.) Introduction to quantum error correction. 
6 Ibid. 
7 Ibid. 
8 Quandela. (n.d.) Quantum Decoherence.

### Page 5

21 October 2026    5

I NSTI TUTE

To fully control qubits for calculations and information transfer, they need to be carefully protected from their surroundings. 
External factors like noise, energy, changes in Earth’s magnetic field, radiation from Wi-Fi or mobile phones or cosmic rays can 
interfere with the process. Even small disturbances can lead to decoherence, making it difficult to develop large-scale, fault-
tolerant quantum computers.9 
According to QuEra Computing, quantum noise can be mitigated through physical isolation of qubits, the development of more 
precise control techniques and quantum error correction, which helps detect and correct errors in physical qubits.  
Exhibit 5: Noise refers to unwanted disturbances, e.g., interactions 
with the environment, that lead to errors in quantum computation

properties, superposition and entanglement

Quantum processing unit (QPU) 
Referred to as the brain of the quantum computer, the QPU is the core hardware where qubits reside and where quantum 
computations take place. QPUs rely on quantum behaviors like superposition and entanglement to tackle problems in areas such 
as cryptography, simulation and optimization. Just as a CPU (central processing unit) in classical computing contains multiple 
components, a QPU includes quantum gates (operations that manipulate qubits in a controlled way, similar to logic gates in 
classical computing) and interconnects, which enable communication between qubits and other quantum processors.10 
Next, we’ll look at broader computing architecture… 
Heterogeneous computing  
Heterogeneous computing refers to a future computing landscape likely combining classical and quantum computing, potentially 
unlocking more powerful, faster and cheaper compute and helping solve problems that classical computers cannot. According to 
BofA Global Research, AI and quantum computing could also converge and complement each other, enabling more 
transformative use cases. 
Quantum modality  
A quantum modality refers to the type of physical technology used to create and manipulate qubits. We discuss the modalities in 
greater depth in the following section. 
Lastly, we outline qubit technologies… 
Neutral atom qubit 
A quantum modality that uses individual atoms with no electrical charge as qubits (Exhibit 7). The atoms are held in place using 
focused lasers known as optical tweezers. Neutral atom qubit systems do not require atoms to be ionized, which means fewer 
energy constraints and potentially simpler cooling requirements. Atoms such as rubidium or cesium are typically used. The qubits 
are manipulated by changing the energy levels or positions of the trapped atoms using laser pulses.

9 QuEra. (n.d.) Quantum Noise. 
10 SpinQ. (2025, January 16). What does a Quantum Computer Look Like? Explain Key Parts.

Exhibit 7: A quantum modality where atoms with a net electrical charge of zero are the foundation for qubits 
Key features of neutral atoms

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Photonic qubit 
A quantum modality that uses particles of light, known as photons, to carry quantum information (Exhibit 8).11 Optical 
components such as beam splitters, which divide light along different paths, and phase shifters, which change the phase of light, 
can be used to perform quantum operations. Photonic quantum computers can use integrated photonic chips to manipulate 
photons and enable quantum logic. 
Exhibit 8: A quantum modality relying on the quantum properties of light 
Key features of photonic qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

11 Moore, J. (2025, March 28). The 6 different types of quantum computing technology. TechTarget.

### Page 7

21 October 2026    7

I NSTI TUTE

Spin qubit 
A quantum modality that uses the intrinsic spin of electrons or nuclei in solid-state materials, such as silicon or diamond. These 
spins can be manipulated using magnetic or electric fields, and their states are read using optical or charge-sensing techniques. 
Spin qubits are particularly appealing because they can leverage existing CMOS (complementary metal-oxide semiconductor; a 
technology to build processors) manufacturing infrastructure, making it easier to integrate them with conventional processors. 
Superconducting qubit 
A quantum modality in which electronic circuits create qubits. They are built using Josephson junctions, where two 
superconductors are separated by a thin insulating layer. Microwave pulses manipulate the qubits’ quantum states to run 
computations.12  
Specifically, a resistance-free current oscillates back and forth around a quantum loop. An injected microwave signal excites the 
current into superposition states.13 To enable this process, superconducting materials are cooled to near absolute zero (around 
10-20 millikelvin) using dilution refrigerators, helping reduce thermal noise and maintain coherence. 
Topological qubit 
A quantum modality that aims to encode quantum information using topological properties rather than relying only on individual 
particle states (Exhibit 9). One way to think about this is through the paths certain quasiparticles take as they move around one 
another, creating “braids” in space-time. Because the information can depend on the overall pattern of these braids rather than 
small details of their paths, the approach is designed to be more resilient to certain disturbances. 
Exhibit 9: Resilient to small perturbations but requires special quasiparticles not yet been conclusively demonstrated in nature 
An infographic of topological qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Trapped-ion qubit  
A quantum modality in which ions (electrically charged atoms) are held in place using electromagnetic fields. These ions act as 
the qubits and are controlled by the system with tightly focused laser beams.14 The qubits are held inside ultra-high vacuum 
(UHV) chambers.

A client-facing version of this report is available at the Deutsche Bank Research 
Portal. The following Deutsche Bank Research analysts contributed to this report: 
Brett Ryan (econ), Binky Chadha and Parag Thatte (equity strategy), Benjamin 
Soff (data centers), Daniel Knauff (software), George Hill (healthcare), Omotayo 
Okusanya (REITs), Peter Abramowitz (office REITs), Mark DeVries (consumer and 
specialty finance), Nicole DeBlase (MI/EE), Corinne Blanchard (clean tech), 
Richa Harnain (transports), and Scott Deuschle (aerospace and defense).  
With the 2026 US midterm elections approaching, we look at key investor 
debates surrounding the event and legislative priorities of the next Congress. 
Despite the uncertainty that midterms can bring, the market appears fairly 
confident on expectations for the overall results. While a surprise Republican-
majority victory in both chambers is still possible, consensus is converging 
around current polling indicating Democrats are likely to flip the seats needed for 
a House majority and may win a Senate majority. Historically, an incumbent 
president has lost seats in 20 of the last 22 midterms. Investor expectations are 
in line with this. Polymarket, for example, is currently above 90% for Democrats 
gaining control of the House and above 60% for Democrats gaining control of the 
Senate.  
With this set of expectations, key investor debates instead focus on the 
legislative agenda for hot-ticket policy issues. Namely, these hinge on AI and 
data centers, affordability (affecting healthcare, housing, and education loans), 
and economic and fiscal policy. Debates also surround possible influence of the 
new Congress on the war in Iran and, by extension, energy prices. In this report, 
we cut through the noise on what the next Congress could reasonably advance 
from these agendas, what the impact would be on affected sectors, and how to 
address market uncertainty – and perceived certainty – into the elections. 
Historically, midterm elections have proven to be a check on administration 
policies. The number of seats lost in the midterms by the incumbent party is tied 
to the president’s approval rating going into midterms, which in turn tends to 
align with consumer confidence. President Trump’s ~38% approval rating, at a 
record low for his tenure, would suggest a little over a 45-seat loss for the 
Republican Party (in the House and Senate combined). That said, polling in the 
Senate races that will determine control of the chamber largely remains within 
the margin of error, and in 2024, Trump won in at least five of the seven core toss-
up states. Thus, the Senate remains a much closer call than the House race.

Source: Real Clear Politics

Source: The Conference Board

Whatever the outcome of the  midterms, the legislative agenda will 
still face constraints from the White House. It is all but impossible for either party 
to gain a veto -proof majority  in either chamber, minimizing the likelihood of a 
large shift in fiscal or regulatory policy. The most  immediately obvious impact of 
Democrats taking the Senate would be the ability to slow or block executive 
branch appointees, including at the Federal Reserve Board of Governors or the 
Supreme Court, should any members step down before their terms end.

### Page 3

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Without garnering a two-thirds majority in both chambers, Democrats lack a clear 
path to unwind the 2025 tax cuts or enact AI-related regulatory legislation – at 
least, not without President Trump’s explicit approval. With respect to the near -
term fiscal policy outlook, the most important period for investors may be the 
“lame duck” session, the period after the November 3 election and before the new 
Congress convenes on January 3. Recall that current federal government funding 
expires on December 11, and the Trump administration is asking for a nearly 60% 
increase in defense spending, the largest since the Korean War.  In addition, the 
debt limit will become binding again around the middle of next year. In short, the 
actions during the lame duck session could determine whether the new Congress 
is seated amid a government shutdown, a contentious battle over the federal 
budget, and the debt limit looming in the background.  
In terms of the market reaction to midterm  elections, the risk-reward profile has 
typically been favorable , with 21 of the last 23 midterm years  seeing positive 
returns in the fourth quarter, with an average gain of 7% in the S&P. The gains are 
notable even after adjusting for typical positive year-end seasonality.

Figure 3: S&P Returns and Midterms

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP

It w ould not be unusual for the market to go sideways as investors hedge 
potential volatility and uncertainty, as we have seen previously ahead of other 
well-telegraphed pivotal events. This has historically been followed by a rally as 
the results became clear and  uncertainty dissipated, regardless of the result 
itself. In the current context, if election polls and prediction market probabilities 
continue being lopsided, the market may not treat the election as an uncertain 
risk event going in. Uncertainty could still ramp up after, and the typical midterm 
election rally may be pushed out. It is notable that the one prominent exception 
to the midterm rallies was in 2018 , when the market sold off sharply after the 
elections as uncertainty spiked around trade war 1.0 as well as Fed policy.

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

We note that the midterm elections also coincide with the third quarter earnings 
season, during which we expect companies to deliver growth of 34%. For next 
year, we expect growth to be a still -robust 17% with risks to the upside. 
Eventually, earnings tend to be the anchor for equities and have been for several 
years despite numerous domestic political as well as geopolitical shocks. 
Across sectors, most have rallied around past midterms in keeping with the 
market, but there isn’t a consistent pattern of out - or underperformance . This 
suggests that for the initial conditions for each sector, specific policies and 
positioning played a bigger role.  For that reason, we turn to a closer analysis of 
key legislative and policy debates impacting exposed sectors.

AI and Data Centers  
AI and data centers have become a campaign talking point for candidates from 
both the Democratic and Republican parties. This has spurred investor questions 
on whether to expect a national regulatory framework constraining data center 
buildouts. Some of th e headlines surrounding this debate overestimate what 
Congress can  accomplish through national legislation compared with what is 
possible at a state level, particularly during a presidential administration 
supportive of AI and data center acceleration. Still, the industry is responding to 
a political backdrop that is increasingly scrutinizing AI and data centers.  
The current administration has prioritized advancing AI and digital infrastructure 
development. The White House ’s AI Action Plan , released July 2025, included a 
set of executive orders supporting the rapid buildout of AI data centers by 
reducing certain federal regulatory and permitting burdens. The administration 
did not preempt state laws on data center infrastructure development and 
federal permitting orders generally do not override state zoning and utility rules. 
In March 2026 , the White House announced the Ratepayer Protection Pledge, 
under which large data center operators would voluntarily commit to covering 
the cost to upgrade generation, transmission, substations, or other necessary 
infrastructure to service these developments.  The industry’s response to these 
proposals has been largely constructive. The Data Center Coalition  (DCC), an 
industry group representing major  hyperscalers and data center  owners and 
operators came out in support of the initial version of the Pledge, while arguing 
that large -load customers across all industries also should cover the costs to 
upgrade energy infrastructure. According to the DCC , “the data center 
industry remains committed to being responsible partners where we  operate, 
which includes  paying our full energy costs  and investing in local jobs and 
workforce development.” 
Congress voted in September 2026 to codify the principles present in the Pledge 
via the Ratepayer Protection Act. The bill, which only required states to “consider” 
these standards, passed overwhelmingly in the House (417-3), but failed to reach 
60 votes in the Senate (57 -43). Critics noted the bill stopped short of imposing 
mandatory nationwide rules. 
Looking forward, we expect a Congress that is more focused on consumer 
protection might look to codify a more stringent version of these rules, requiring 
large-load customers to bear the full incremental costs associated with 
generation, transmission, and distribution upgrades.  However, even if such a 
framework were passed into law, we believe the overall economic impact to large 
data center operators would be relatively modest, for two reasons. 
For one, the White House would maintain a veto over  bills that include language 
it views as overly burdensome towards AI infrastructure development . Two, data 
center operators  generally underwrite projects only when they have a high 
degree of confidence in securing power and, where necessary, may already be 
embedding the cost of upgrading energy infrastructure into their investment 
plans. We expect developers to be capable of passing on sustained increases in 
power infrastructure costs to their customers , which, for many 100  MW+ 
developments, are the major investment-grade hyperscalers.

### Page 6

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 6

Though data center development is receiving increasing national attention, it is 
still largely a local issue, and the most consequential elections will be at the state 
and local levels. We have seen a number of state -driven actions on data center s 
in recent months, including: 
• In July, New York State’s governor issued an executive order to establish 
a temporary moratorium on data center development  while the state 
develops a standardized framework of rules for key issues (including 
responsibilities for power and energy infrastructure upgrades; additional 
power generation; clean energy; water use, emission, and waste 
management; and state/local financ ial incentives). The New York 
legislature also passed a one -year moratorium on Department of 
Environmental Conservation permits and approvals for 20  MW+ data 
center developments. 
• In August, Texas’ governor directed the state’s public utility commission 
to conduct a comprehensive audit of every data center advancing 
through the ERCOT (Texas’s grid operator) interconnection process. The 
audit will cover several notable areas including public financial support, 
electricity demand and supply, water use, community impacts, and 
ownership and control. 
• In September, California’s governor passed a comprehensive regulatory 
package, covering electricity, water, reporting, and environmental 
review. Most of these provisions take effect January 1, 2027, and phase 
in through 2027 -2028, but the package does not ban data centers or 
impose a statewide moratorium on development activity. 
• Oregon, Pennsylvania, New Jersey, and other states have also 
established orders to create standards and frameworks governing data 
center development (but without imposing broad moratoriums). 
At the more local level, a large number of moratoriums have been passed on new 
data center development. Data collected by SemiAnalysis, an independent 
research firm, shows that, as of September, more than 300 local data  center 
moratoriums have been adopted nationwide across counties, municipalities, 
townships, and other local governments.  
However, these state and local moratoriums appear  thus far to be less impactful 
to near- and medium-term development activity than headline coverage might 
otherwise suggest. According to SemiAnalysis, only about 2.3 GW of the 38 GW 
of data center capacity planned or under construction for 2027 has faced delays 
as a result of  the New York state executive order (0.8 GW) and/or local 
moratoriums (1.5 GW). 
We believe the primary reasons that moratoriums are, in reality, less likely to 
impact near -term development activity are (1) that these moratoriums largely 
impact new projects and applications (rather than existing developments in  
progress), (2) there may be flexibility to relocate or rezone early-stage projects in 
response to moratoriums, and (3) data center developers are increasingly 
partnering with local communities to gain their support via investments and other 
programs (as we wrote about recently).  Potential regulatory risk has not yet 
slowed capex spending patterns, with capex spend still expected to climb from

### Page 7

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 7

$0.5 trillion in 2025 to $1.3 trillion by 2027; data centers typically represent ~20-
30% of the total bill of materials for new AI capacity.  
Although the practical impact on near- and medium-term development pipelines 
appears less pronounced than headlines might suggest, all this is not to say that 
data centers will not face increasing local opposition following the midterms. 
Indeed, i n our conversations with industry participants, there is an 
acknowledgment that greater public scrutiny of data centers is not going away.  
Additional moratoriums, zoning restrictions, permitting requirements, or utility -
related conditions could affect the pace, cost , and overall feasibility of future 
projects.
